### Decision history policy

- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Server / the Vexing Arrow had been serialized in the world's entity-region before the XtraArrows access fix existed; loading chunk [-411, -122] instantiated that old entity, its first tick still entered the original illegal protected-method call before any player command could remove it, and the server therefore crashed immediately whenever the chunk became loaded / in testing`. User direction: inspect the fresh MineRENT start and remove the old arrow if it was still crashing the server. Resolution mechanism under test: identify its exact one-entity chunk in `world/entities/r.-13.-4.mca`, remove only that chunk record, upload and verify the cleaned region, restore Forge's error-removal switch to `false`, boot to `Done`, remove the temporary force-load and save the world.
- 2026-10-06 — User direction: manual SMP event administration, related `/smp` recovery commands, and `/jemtier` must belong exclusively to the player `Sirio_o`, with no OP bypass. Codex choice: replace permission-level predicates with an exact server-side player-identity predicate on the complete `smp event` and `jemtier` command roots, protect both boss-revival aliases, and leave participant-facing SMP GUI, party, voting, return and exit commands unchanged.

- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Server / party event startup stored return data in event-specific members and then independently teleported every participant, including the host, to arena slots; the host therefore lost the GUI start position, ordinary bosses had a separate return owner, and completion could reuse uncleared coordinates from a previous attempt / in testing`. User direction: keep the host in place, move accepted members to the host, save each exact pre-teleport position, privately offer Return/Stay for Boss, Raid, Blood Moon and future party events, and scope the data to one launch. Codex choice: add one party-owned teleport/return lifecycle with a per-launch UUID, reuse it from every hosted event, make stale chat buttons invalid, and clear arena access plus each player's return record after their choice.
- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Both Sided / the Blood Moon virtual wall delegated the full movement vector to vanilla multi-axis collision resolution, so horizontal contact could also reduce vertical motion and hold a jumping player down; projectile crossing then rewound arrows to xo/yo/zo while preserving the old side flag, causing through-block arrows to cross again on following ticks and repeatedly emit position/velocity synchronization / in testing`. User direction: walls only physically block passage and arrows must stop or rebound without recursion, collision loops, entity spam, or packet spam. Codex choice: resolve only X/Z against the wall while preserving Y exactly, let accepted party members pass, and stop arrows once at the calculated boundary point with a short re-entry guard while non-arrow projectiles receive one damped reflection.
- 2026-10-06 — User direction: map Event/Boss travel must always land outside restrictions on a safe above-ground surface, never in caves or inside blocks, while campaign and miniboss gates remain authoritative. Codex choice: allow inside-map travel only for Resource Rush and make all outside searches use the motion-blocking surface heightmap even when the arena itself has constrained structure Y bounds.

- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Client Distribution / pointUpdateAlwaysInclude matched only kubejs/assets/**/lang/ru_ru.json, so package_delta copied 64 localization files while the remaining 14 KubeJS scripts, data files, configs, examples and README were never selected; the ZIP writer only archives the selected staging tree, therefore the otherwise valid 00-23-42 point-update could not install the complete recipient runtime / resolved`. User direction: every point-update must contain the entire current KubeJS tree. Resolution mechanism: changed the canonical include pattern to `kubejs/**/*`, reissued the published delta through `repackage-release`, verified all 78 Client KubeJS files are present, updated baseline package metadata, and removed the incomplete package from the updates directory.
- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Both Sided / Xtra Arrows 3.0.12 invoked Entity.updateInWaterStateAndDoFluidPushing from ArrowsAPI.vexTick even though that Minecraft method is protected; the Vexing Arrow reached this call on its first entity tick, the JVM access check threw IllegalAccessError before the arrow could finish ticking, and the uncaught ticking-entity exception terminated both the integrated client world and dedicated server / in testing`. User direction: stop the through-block XtraArrows arrow from crashing client and server. Codex choice: widen only the exact vanilla method through JEMCompat's Forge access transformer, preserving XtraArrows' native arrow behavior without replacing its tick logic.
- 2026-10-06 — `Bug / Both Sided / the Blood Moon wall was appended to Entity.collide only after vanilla had already resolved movement; this made the virtual wall depend on that private collision return path while the modded movement pipeline still owned the requested move, so a player could cross and later re-enter the rendered red boundary / in testing`. User direction: the red wall must behave as a solid block in both directions. Codex choice: constrain the requested Vec3 at the head of Entity.move and then let the complete vanilla/modded movement pipeline process the already wall-safe delta, retaining collision-only behavior with no correction teleport.
- 2026-10-06 — User direction: Wave 3 and Wave 5 must leave at least three seconds with no attack field, rapid combinations must also pause between attacks, and their danger preview must last twice as long. Codex applied one 60-tick recovery invariant to ordinary and combination impacts and increased combination telegraph from 16 to 32 ticks while retaining the existing 12-tick impact VFX and per-pattern cooldowns.
- 2026-10-06 — Removed `jem-villager-talking-1.0.0-dev.jar` from the clean distributed Client, local dedicated Server, and Server Test because the user explicitly excluded Talking Villager from the next client release. It was already absent from MineRENT. After initially pausing deployment, the user explicitly restored authorization, so the removal shipped with the 00:31 MineRENT release.

- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Both Sided / fishing-event XP called ServerPlayer.giveExperiencePoints directly, so no ExperienceOrb existed to invoke vanilla repairPlayerItems; Mending therefore never selected an equipped damaged item, no XP was consumed for repair, and the full reward went straight to the XP bar / in testing`. User direction: fishing XP must repair only worn/equipped Mending armor. Codex choice: expose vanilla `ExperienceOrb.repairPlayerItems` through a narrow invoker and grant only its remainder directly, preserving vanilla equipped-item selection and repair ratio without dropping an orb.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Server / boss XP remained as world orbs while hosted-event completion distributed loot and returned participants; the player left the arena before collecting the orb, so the native boss reward existed but never reached that player / in testing`. User direction: award boss XP directly instead of leaving it on the floor. Codex choice: intercept the native XP amount for hosted and Blood Moon set-piece bosses, run the same vanilla Mending path, grant the remainder directly to every eligible participant, and zero the floor drop.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Server / ordinary death used vanilla XP loss because no non-event death policy copied the original player's three XP fields; cloning created the replacement player with reset values, then vanilla XP drops represented only the lost portion, so the previous level could not survive respawn / in testing`. User direction: ordinary deaths outside events preserve XP. Codex choice: suppress only non-event player XP drops and copy level, progress, and total XP during death clone, leaving event-owned death rules isolated.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Both Sided / Blood Moon boundary enforced crossing by changing position after movement on both server and client; the server teleported to a saved point while the client independently clamped to the wall, repeated disagreement triggered more corrections, and the player appeared sucked into the boundary without being released / in testing`. User direction: the closed wall must act like a solid block with no edge teleport. Codex choice: remove Blood Moon post-move corrections and add four full-height collision shapes to normal Entity collision; the wall is passable during group arrival and after completion, then solid for both directions.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Server / Blood Moon startup called awaitEntry instead of the shared teleportParticipants path; prepare calculated every party member's safe arrival but awaitEntry never used those coordinates, so only players who walked into the arena could advance startup and remote accepted members stayed at their old positions / in testing`. User direction: all party members teleport to the host event on start. Codex choice: use the existing encounter-owned participant teleport and keep the pre-event return coordinates recorded by initialize.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Server / protected Blood Moon death saved the complete inventory and later also serialized the same generated entity drops into safeDrops; restore loaded the full snapshot first and then delivered safeDrops separately, so equipped armor could be restored twice after defeat / in testing`. User direction: remove the observed armor duplication. Codex choice: make the full inventory snapshot canonical, discard matching death drops when it exists, and retain safeDrops only as backward-compatible recovery for records without a snapshot.
- 2026-10-05 — `Bug / Server / Arena 5 applied the shared 2.00 post-scaling multiplier to every boss-owned damage source and multiplied its 35% safety cap by the same value; this produced 4.20 native damage scaling and a 70% max-health cap, so all attacks remained 100/100 of the previous strength / in testing`. User direction: when the old result is 100 the new result must be 70 for every attack. Codex choice: multiply both damage and cap by `0.70`, changing the shared Arena 5 multiplier from `2.00` to `1.40`, final native coefficient from `4.20` to `2.94`, and cap from 70% to 49%.
- 2026-10-05 — Implemented the user's raid-like Wave 2 roster with pillagers, evokers, ravagers and Skarrier Mobs Rager; event-owned hostile mobs in one attempt can neither damage nor target each other, leaving accepted players as their combat targets. The second batch adds Carnager to the candidate roster only when Skarrier Mobs' own `CarnagerEntitySpawningConditionProcedure` reports its world-wide `WitherDefeated` state. Codex independently reused that installed-mod gate rather than reconstructing a per-player advancement check.

- 2026-10-04 22:51 +05:00 — `Bug / Client / the girlfriend could not join because the numeric MineRENT fallback was incorrectly supplied with the primary domain port: MineRENT exposes ForeverSMP.minerent.io:25565 through its hostname route but the live Network page assigns 87.228.57.196:31604 to direct fallback access; opening either address as HTTPS additionally sends non-Minecraft traffic, and the console showed Connectivity eventually ignoring repeated malformed traffic from her IP / diagnosed, awaiting retry with the correct in-game address`. The user explicitly asked for a browser-side check after the supplied numeric address failed; Codex independently inspected the authenticated MineRENT console and Network page and added a project rule requiring live network-page verification instead of deriving a numeric endpoint from DNS.
- Updated the project `AGENTS.md` because the user explicitly required every project-changing prompt to record both what changed and why. The user supplied the technical direction that changelog entries must separate their requested behavior and prescribed implementation constraints from technical choices made independently by Codex; Codex encoded that distinction as a mandatory project rule.
- Added mandatory structured bug records because the user explicitly required `Bug / Client, Server, or Both Sided / concrete cause / status`. The user supplied the technical direction to derive the cause through five whys, use `in testing` before approval, and reserve `resolved` for behavior they have approved; Codex kept this project-specific rather than changing unrelated global workflows.

### Superseded before approval

- `Bug / Both Sided / selective grindstone removed at most one non-curse enchantment, returned experience instead of books, and had no durability transaction / superseded before approval`
  - Five whys: the requested books were absent because result pickup only consumed the input; pickup only consumed the input because the selective code changed the vanilla result stack but had no result-slot completion hook; there was no completion hook because the first implementation reused only `createResult`; `createResult` cannot award secondary outputs because it runs for preview recalculation on both sides; therefore the real missing owner was a server-side result-slot transaction tied to successful pickup.
  - User request and technical direction at that time: keep the ordinary grindstone screen usable, add a localized dropdown with multi-selection, preserve unselected and modded enchantments, return removed enchantments as books, charge 5% maximum durability per removed enchantment, include curses, warn before the item breaks, and never lose books when inventory is full.
  - Codex technical choice at that time: keep vanilla behavior when nothing is selected; mirror dropdown selection in the existing menu button protocol; construct the preview from a copied `ItemStack`; finish atomically from the grindstone result slot; return one enchanted book per selected enchantment; place books in inventory or safely drop them; when the durability payment breaks the source item, make the first book the result-slot item so the transaction still has a valid pickup.
  - Supersession reason: the user explicitly rejected this unapproved batch-selection/result-slot interaction and replaced it with direct drag-and-drop extraction from real book slots.

- `Bug / Client / JEI crashed during recipe synchronization because the selective-disenchantment Mixin treated JEI's virtual grindstone as a live player menu / superseded before approval`
  - Five whys: the client closed while joining because Forge's `RecipesUpdatedEvent` listener failed during JEI startup; the listener failed immediately after vanilla anvil enumeration because JEI next generated grindstone recipes through a cached synthetic `GrindstoneMenu`; JEM changed that synthetic menu because its Mixin applied custom slots, result suppression, and enchantment refresh to every grindstone instance; JEI's synthetic menu is identified by reserved `containerId = 0`, while real player-opened container ids are non-zero; the concrete root cause was the missing lifecycle boundary between JEI recipe simulation and a real interactive grindstone menu.
  - Evidence chain: the 2026-10-04 12:11 client crash reached `ForgeHooksClient.onRecipesUpdated` through JEI's `StartEventObserver`; the current log completed all 40 pre-existing `spawn:blue_footed_boots` anvil warnings and then stopped before `Registering recipes: jei:minecraft took`; the earlier 11:09 successful log contained the same 40 warnings but completed JEI; installed JEI 15.49.0.194 bytecode creates its fake grindstone with `new GrindstoneMenu(0, inventory)` immediately after anvil recipe generation.
  - User report and technical direction: the user reported `crashed` after the Server Test deploy. The user identified the failed outcome but did not prescribe a technical implementation.
  - Codex technical choice at that time: preserve JEM selective disenchantment only for non-zero real container sessions and leave `containerId = 0` virtual grindstones fully vanilla, so JEI could calculate its recipe catalog without synthetic book slots or JEM result suppression while the player's server-authoritative grindstone behavior remained unchanged.
  - Supersession reason: the user rejected the entire selective-disenchantment feature before approving this compatibility fix, so the owning mixins and all integrations were removed instead of retaining a special JEI branch.

- `Bug / Both Sided / selective disenchantment used checkbox-like rows and deferred the real operation until result pickup instead of letting each enchantment book be taken directly / superseded before approval`
  - Five whys: enchantment books could not be dragged because the left UI rendered text rows rather than container slots; the rows were only selectors because the menu stored a set of chosen enchantments; the sword was not mutated on row interaction because removal was deferred to the vanilla result preview and pickup lifecycle; that lifecycle necessarily bundled selected books into one later transaction; the concrete root cause was that the result slot, rather than each enchantment book slot, owned the disenchantment operation.
  - User request and technical direction: completely remove the current selection/result behavior; keep one `Снять зачарование` button; open one book column to the left of the main GUI; make every enchantment an individually draggable book; remove that exact enchantment and immediately charge 5% of maximum durability when its book is taken; close the temporary presentation with the menu; and show `Ваш предмет сломается, если вы снимете данные чары` whenever less than one removal cost remains. Every product behavior and the direct book-to-enchantment mapping came from the user.
  - Codex technical choice at that time: append five real scrolling menu slots backed by a synthetic view container; make slot removal the atomic server-authoritative extraction point; keep curses and modded enchantments through `EnchantmentHelper`; suppress only the competing single-item vanilla disenchantment result while preserving two-item grindstone repair; use real enchanted-book `ItemStack` rendering, enchantment names, per-row `−5%`, a scrollbar, persistent red panel text, and one action-bar warning; disable shift-click on synthetic slots so an inventory-full quick move could not mutate the sword without a destination.
  - Supersession reason: the user explicitly classified the complete mechanic as unacceptable and ordered its complete removal before approval.

- Removed JEM selective disenchantment completely because the user explicitly said `Убирай ... снятие чар completely` and rejected the mechanic as a whole. The user's product direction was complete removal with no replacement behavior; the user did not prescribe a technical removal method. Codex independently chose to restore unmodified vanilla grindstone ownership by deleting the custom menu, book-slot, extraction and screen classes; removing the server/common and client mixins plus their accessors; unregistering the client screen hook; deleting feature-only localization keys; and removing the obsolete owning documentation. No alternative disenchantment system was added.

### In testing

- `Bug / Both Sided / every XtraArrows Vexing Arrow projectile calls protected Entity.m_20073_ during its first loaded tick, so a saved projectile crashes the dedicated server whenever its chunk becomes active / in testing`
  - Five whys: the server crashed because a saved Diamond Vexing Arrow tick threw `IllegalAccessError`; the tick failed because `ArrowsAPI.vexTick` directly invoked protected `Entity.m_20073_`; the illegal call remained reachable because all five material variants share the same Vexing Arrow implementation; the projectile survived restarts because entities are persisted in the chunk independently of their inventory item; the concrete root cause was XtraArrows 3.0.12 shipping runtime bytecode that accesses a protected Minecraft method from an unrelated class.
  - User request and technical direction: remove the weapon and all its variants through Item Obliterator and create a new world instead of retaining or repairing the old world.
  - Codex technical choice: blacklist `flint_vexing_arrow`, `iron_vexing_arrow`, `golden_vexing_arrow`, `diamond_vexing_arrow`, and `netherite_vexing_arrow`; remove the old world from the active path so its saved projectile and forced chunk cannot load; and let Forge generate a clean replacement world.

- `Bug / Server / Blood Moon blocked non-party observers at its wall and Ancient Warden could still investigate or navigate toward players who were not active combat participants / in testing`
  - Five whys: safe observers could not enter because player collision treated every non-party player as an outsider even after `combatStarted`; that classification remained because wall passability was derived only from accepted party membership; eliminated and unrelated players were already excluded from `EventSession.active`, but that exclusion only protected ordinary target assignment; Ancient Warden could still react because vibration processing and anger memory happen before or without a `LivingChangeTargetEvent`; the concrete root cause was that Blood Moon mixed two separate contracts—free player traversal and combat target eligibility—and enforced only the latter at the generic target-change seam instead of at Warden perception and persistent AI memory.
  - User request and technical direction: after Blood Moon combat starts, every player may freely enter and leave, including eliminated players and observers outside the active party; all non-event mobs must be removed from the arena; only Blood Moon event mobs remain; and every player outside the active participant set must be completely ignored by event mobs, explicitly including Ancient Warden hearing, investigation and navigation and Wave 3/5 bosses.
  - Codex technical choice: keep the pre-combat participant gate, make the rendered wall passable to every player once combat starts, retain the existing continuous ambient-mob ejection, centralize Blood Moon target eligibility on the event participant set, reject ineligible targets at the Forge target event and Warden `canTargetEntity`, and clear stale Warden anger, attack, roar, disturbance and walk memories when no eligible target owns them.

- `Bug / Server / Blood Moon crashed the dedicated server while constructing the temporary Arena 3 floor because its randomized floor comparator returned different ordering results for the same positions during one TimSort / in testing`
  - Five whys: the dedicated server stopped because `ArrayList.sort` threw `Comparison method violates its general contract!`; TimSort rejected the comparator because repeated comparisons of the same two floor positions could reverse their order; their order could reverse because the comparison key called `level.random.nextDouble()` every time the comparator ran; the random value was recomputed because floor distance and visual jitter were combined inside the comparator instead of captured with the floor position; the concrete root cause was a stateful randomized comparator in `BloodMoonSetPieces.buildArena` whose result was neither stable nor transitive.
  - User request and technical direction: the user supplied the complete production server log showing the failure at `BloodMoonSetPieces.buildArena:987` during Blood Moon progression. The user did not prescribe a repair algorithm or change the approved arena-floor behavior.
  - Codex technical choice: preserve the existing center-out floor order and `0.55` random visual jitter, but calculate one immutable ordering value per `BlockPos` before sorting and make the comparator read only those captured values, so TimSort receives a stable transitive ordering while the authored floor spread remains unchanged.

- `Bug / Both Sided / independent per-event cooldowns let one physical incident emit several competing reactions, while the client stored only one pending line per villager and immediately replaced the shared HUD subtitle / in testing`
  - Five whys: villagers discussed night and work during a zombie attack because every detected condition was submitted independently; the submissions competed because cooldown and speaker occupancy were keyed by event id rather than by the local situation; one zombie produced several submissions because generic danger, entity join and per-villager perception treated the same entity as separate incidents; multi-speaker lines then disappeared because the client map replaced a villager's pending line and any immediate packet replaced the shared subtitle; the concrete root cause was the absence of one server-owned local attention scene combined with a lossy client pending-line map.
  - User request and technical direction: react to only one major situation at a time, make threats outrank night and workplace complaints, stop rapid speech and line-pool exhaustion, preserve readable multi-villager dialogue with labelled authors while solo lines remain unlabelled, explicitly name each reaction's subject, use the supplied door, village-block and Creative-mode wording, and make first witch encounters and child reactions visibly alarmed.
  - Codex technical choice: derive cross-event attention from the existing authored reaction priority; allow only a strictly higher-priority local incident to interrupt; reserve the scene for its complete three-second-per-line sequence plus five seconds of silence; add speaker-wide occupancy; retain every pending multi-speaker line in an ordered client list; cancel an old queue only when a new immediate scene arrives; delay a real remembered-workstation path failure for thirty seconds; remove the duplicate inaccessible-workstation emission; and clarify the matching danger, blocked-door, torch and regeneration lines alongside the user's five examples.

- Replaced the legacy villager trigger catalog with the user-supplied Villager Speech V2 contract because the user explicitly required all 417 semantic events, 1083 authored reactions, transition-only state activation, local perception and knowledge boundaries, namespace-neutral modpack handling, explainable selection logs, and an automated 417/417 coverage gate. The user additionally required that HUD dialogue identify its speakers while solo lines stay visually unlabelled. Codex independently kept `JEMVillagerTalking` as the canonical owner, loaded the supplied JSON as the strict runtime registry, registered every catalog event through its declared production adapter strategy, separated occurrence/state/perception/ambient tracking, bounded local scans and runtime memories, retained server-authoritative selection with per-event cooldowns, added an optional World Tier event adapter without loading optional classes, and extended the packet/HUD contract so multi-speaker scenes use distinct villagers and render a custom name or localized profession plus scene ordinal for every line. The supplied XLSX remains the human review view; runtime reads only the supplied JSON source of truth.

- `Bug / Both Sided / manually damageable equipment lost durability and its vanilla bar because the production compatibility JAR omitted the Mixin refmap required to bind the durability hooks after obfuscation / in testing`
  - Five whys: Duskfire armor and the other configured originally-indestructible equipment had neither damage loss nor a durability bar because `ItemStack.isDamageableItem` and `ItemStack.getMaxDamage` stayed vanilla; those methods stayed vanilla because `InfiniteEquipmentDurabilityMixin` failed to find its production targets; the bar correction failed for the same reason because `ItemDurabilityBarMixin` could not find `Item.getBarWidth` or `Item.getBarColor`; both target lookups failed because `jemcompat.mixins.json` requested `jemcompat.refmap.json` but the deployed `jemcompat-3.7.0.jar` did not contain that file; the concrete root cause was that the JEMCompat build treated the generated refmap as optional and could successfully package an obfuscated production JAR without the mapping metadata on which both durability mixins depend.
  - User request and technical direction: restore durability loss and the ordinary vanilla durability bar for every previously configured weapon and armor item that originally had no durability, retain all existing maximum-durability values, prevent the bar from stretching across the GUI, and verify Duskfire armor as the concrete example. After the server-only limitation was identified, the user explicitly authorized updating both the client compatibility JAR and the server boundary fix.
  - Codex technical choice: keep the existing equipment eligibility and durability values unchanged; rebuild the missing refmap through the existing Mixin annotation processor; declare that refmap as a required `compileJava` output; and make JEMCompat's `jar` task fail when the archive does not contain it, so an obfuscated compatibility JAR can no longer silently ship with both durability hooks disabled.

- `Bug / Server / Blood Moon boundary trapped an active participant against the wall and repeatedly overrode movement and camera rotation / in testing`
  - Five whys: the player could not step away or rotate freely because the server sent a corrective teleport every tick; the correction repeated because the next accepted movement packet crossed the boundary again immediately; it crossed immediately because `HostedBoundary` saved the last inside position all the way up to the collision plane and restored that edge-adjacent coordinate; the trap became stronger because every correction discarded the player's complete velocity instead of only the component crossing the wall; the concrete root cause was an unsafe edge-intersecting return point combined with per-tick full-motion reset in the server-owned hosted-boundary loop.
  - User request and technical direction: the user reported the Blood Moon wall trapping movement and camera rotation and explicitly required a server-only fix so clients do not need an update. The server-only compatibility constraint is the user's technical direction; the user did not prescribe clearance distance, state ownership, or velocity handling.
  - Codex technical choice: preserve the approved client renderer and network contract unchanged; classify ordinary inside positions with the full player collision box; persist return coordinates only while the player's feet position is an additional `0.75` blocks inside that valid box; reject previously persisted edge points; project a missing or unsafe point to the same inset box; preserve current yaw and pitch; and remove only motion on axes actually corrected by the one-time server teleport.

- Increased ordinary Blood Moon mob movement speed by 6% on Wave 1, 12% on Wave 2, and 15% on Wave 4 because the user explicitly supplied those wave-specific percentages before the MineRENT release. The exact percentages and affected waves are the user's technical direction. Codex independently kept the existing server-authoritative `BloodMoonWaves` balance owner, applied one named `wave_speed` attribute modifier through the shared ordinary-mob spawn pipeline, allowed it to compose with existing tier/archetype/elite modifiers, and excluded Wave 3/5 set-piece bosses because those waves have separate authored movement profiles.

- Added a rapid three-attack combination to every Arena 3 and Arena 5 boss encounter because the user explicitly required a special chain every 20–30 seconds while preserving ordinary attack cadence. The user's technical direction fixed exactly three quickly consecutive attacks, a warning slightly below one second, roughly 0.6 seconds of attack effect, immediate handoff to the next pattern, and unchanged ordinary cooldown behavior. Codex independently implemented a persistent server-owned queue of three distinct chain-safe patterns, selected a random 400–600 tick interval, set combo telegraphs to 16 ticks and impact VFX to 12 ticks, began the next warning 2 ticks after impact so the visible effects can overlap into an approximately three-second chain, applied each used pattern's normal cooldown, and excluded final, cover-dependent, boss-movement, healing, and twin-invulnerability mechanics from the shortened sequence.

- `Bug / Client / approved filled event boundaries appeared with Complementary but disappeared in vanilla rendering and other shader packs / in testing`
  - Five whys: the same server packet and filled quad geometry existed in every renderer mode, but visibility changed with the active shader path; it changed because boundary vertices were submitted through the shared textured entity-translucent-emissive material; that material required texture, light, overlay, and normal inputs even though the wall is pure vertex color; shader packs remap that entity shader differently, so Complementary happened to preserve the wall while other paths could discard or neutralize it; the concrete root cause was coupling a plain translucent boundary surface to a pack-dependent textured emissive entity shader instead of a buffered untextured color material.
  - User request and technical direction: preserve the now-approved appearance exactly, but make it visible without shaders and with shader packs other than Complementary. The user explicitly approved and requested locking the continuous filled full-height wall behavior.
  - Codex technical choice: lock the geometry, color, opacity, double-sided rendering, world-space placement, and no-wireframe contract; move only boundaries to a dedicated `POSITION_COLOR` quad `RenderType` with normal alpha blending, `LEQUAL`, color-only writes, `NO_CULL`, and the existing Oculus shadow-pass exclusion; keep attack telegraphs on their separate entity-compatible material.

- Added three shared attack patterns to every Arena 3 and Arena 5 boss scenario because the user approved increasing each existing five-pattern set to eight and required less safe space in old and new attacks. The user's product direction defined three additional patterns, smaller openings, and preservation of dodgeable telegraphs. Codex independently authored `CONSTRICTING_HALO`, `TRIPLE_RIFT`, and `HUNTER_LATTICE`; reused the one immutable `AttackGeometry` and cyan renderer pipeline; tightened row, grid, ring, cross, corridor, quadrant, and radial geometries toward roughly 60–80% threat coverage; and limited normal continuous safe pockets to approximately 4–6 blocks without creating unavoidable full-arena damage.

- Locked the full-height filled event-boundary presentation because the user explicitly said the result is finally correct and asked to protect it. The user's approved behavior is a continuous colored translucent wall across the complete vertical boundary with no wireframe, rails, gaps, terrain truncation, or camera-relative duplicate. Codex independently scoped the lock to the boundary geometry and packet contract while allowing only narrow material/backend/shader-compatibility changes that preserve the approved appearance.

- Added persistent combat-event reconnect handling because the user explicitly required a player who disconnects during an event to be able to return to the same solo encounter, while an absence longer than five minutes must count as that player's defeat without stopping the event. The user's technical direction defined the five-minute threshold, solo arena return, individual defeat, and continued event lifecycle. Codex independently kept the canonical `EncounterContext` as owner, stored wall-clock disconnect time inside the existing participant record, applied the timeout to every participant, teleported only a reconnecting solo participant to the shared collision-checked arena respawn, marked an expired participant `eliminated`, deferred the localized defeat notice and origin return until login, and deliberately avoided `failAttempt` so bosses, waves, and remaining participants continue unchanged.

- `Bug / Server / ordinary Arena 3 and Arena 5 boss effects could reach players every six seconds or faster when several bosses were alive / in testing`
  - Five whys: an ordinary debuff could recur after six seconds because the minimum cast cooldown was hardcoded to `120` ticks; multi-boss scenarios could feel even faster because every boss stored and advanced its own `NEXT_CAST`; each boss also cast immediately during initialization because profile assignment and cast scheduling were coupled per entity; the encounter therefore had no shared throttle for player-facing debuffs; the concrete root cause was per-boss scheduling for an effect stream that players experience as one encounter-wide channel.
  - User request and technical direction: the user required a longer cooldown between ordinary effects and explicitly excluded thunder strikes, which should remain unchanged. The user identified the six-second cadence but did not prescribe a replacement interval or scheduler ownership.
  - Codex technical choice: keep Zeus thunder on its existing independent seven-second system; move ordinary debuffs to one server-owned encounter schedule; allow one immediate opening debuff; select one living profiled boss as each later caster; and use a 12–20 second interval so additional bosses cannot multiply effect frequency.

- `Bug / Client / full-height event borders still looked like wireframe rails instead of continuous translucent surfaces / in testing`
  - Five whys: the deployed border looked like lines because the horizontal rails and vertical posts were rendered at alpha `154–222` while the wall face used only alpha `18–34`; the accents therefore visually dominated the face even though the buffer already received four vertices per side; the face was so faint because the previous full-height implementation divided the pulse alpha by eight and capped it at roughly 13% opacity; the bright grid had been added to communicate chunk scale after replacing the short terrain ribbon; the concrete root cause was a presentation hierarchy that made auxiliary grid geometry stronger than the already-correct rasterized quad geometry.
  - User request and technical direction: the user explicitly required rasterized faces occupying the complete area between border vertices, one continuous filled translucent wall without wireframe, line thickening, debug/AABB rendering, gaps, or one-sided disappearance; requested `QUADS` or paired triangles, 20–35% opacity, full `minY–maxY` height, and a final source-level check that the consumer emits faces rather than edges.
  - Codex technical choice: retain the shader-compatible `NEW_ENTITY` material contract, `VertexFormat.Mode.QUADS`, color-only writes, normal alpha blending, `LEQUAL` depth test, full-bright wall normals, Oculus shadow-pass exclusion, and `NO_CULL`; remove every rail, post, and grid submission; emit exactly four continuous filled side quads with a restrained 20–35% alpha pulse for all event and hosted-boss boundaries.

- `Bug / Server / Arena 3 kept the natural floor and snow while the Arena 5 set-piece received a temporary authored floor / in testing`
  - Five whys: Arena 3 never replaced blocks because `buildArena` returned immediately for every wave except the final wave; it could not safely reuse the remaining method because `floorPalette` explicitly rejected every Wave 3 scenario; snow therefore remained because the shared thin-cover removal ran only after a Wave 5 floor queue existed; restoration also recognized only Wave 5 because `finishWave` checked the final-wave number; the concrete root cause was a hardcoded Wave 5 gate around an otherwise reusable snapshot, snow-removal, paced replacement, and restoration lifecycle.
  - User request and technical direction: the user required Arena 3 to use the same block-replacement approach as Arena 5, remove snow during the arena presentation, and restore every block to its previous state for the following wave. The user prescribed lifecycle parity but left the scenario palettes and source structure to Codex.
  - Codex technical choice: run the existing bounded floor queue for both set-piece waves; reuse installed thematic palettes where the scenarios share a visual family; use vanilla Nether blocks for Red Knights and mud blocks for Tremor; snapshot both floor and thin cover before mutation; and restore every exact saved `BlockState` when Wave 3 or Wave 5 finishes and during cleanup.

- Reduced Arena 3 and Arena 5 attack cooldown duration by 68% because the user explicitly required both set-piece arenas to attack faster. The user's numeric direction is that 32% of every previous cooldown remains. Codex independently applied that factor to initial attack availability, per-pattern reuse cooldown, and the shared post-impact recovery gate while preserving windup and telegraph duration so increased cadence does not remove the dodge warning.

- `Bug / Client / Blood Moon and Resource Rush borders stopped a few blocks above the terrain instead of covering the full height of every event chunk / in testing`
  - Five whys: the screenshots showed only a short wall because the client rendered every perimeter segment with a fixed height of `3.25` blocks; that fixed height started from a separately sampled surface Y for each block; surface sampling existed because the previous presentation was designed as a terrain-following ribbon; the renderer kept using that ribbon even though the server packet already supplied authoritative dimension `minY` and `maxY`; the concrete root cause was duplicated vertical-boundary ownership in which client heightmap projection discarded the server-owned world-height bounds.
  - User request and technical direction: the user required the border to cover the full height of all chunks occupied by the event and supplied screenshots demonstrating the short red and yellow walls. The full-height product behavior is the user's direction; the user did not prescribe vertex layout, opacity, grid spacing, or rendering helpers.
  - Codex technical choice at that stage: replace the per-block terrain ribbon for Blood Moon and Resource Rush with four depth-tested walls from packet `minY` through `maxY`; retain a subtle translucent fill; and add bright horizontal and vertical rails every sixteen blocks so chunk sections remain readable without generating hundreds of terrain segments. The user subsequently rejected the grid-dominant presentation as wireframe, so the full-height geometry remains but the rails and posts are superseded by the continuous filled-wall implementation recorded above.

- `Bug / Client / Arena 3 boss attacks used a different visual palette from the approved Arena 5 telegraphs / in testing`
  - Five whys: Arena 3 looked unlike Arena 5 because the server sent orange as its telegraph and impact base color while Arena 5 sent cyan; that base color changed the entire presentation because the shared renderer derives fill, dark support outline, light accent, sweep, impact core, and trail from it; the palette differed because `wave == 3` selected a separate hardcoded color in both packet paths; the split persisted even though both waves already shared the same geometry snapshots and client renderer; the concrete root cause was a wave-specific palette fork inside the server's otherwise shared attack-visual contract.
  - User request and technical direction: the user explicitly identified Arena 5 telegraphs as the desired presentation and required Arena 3 to look equally beautiful and consistent. Reusing the Arena 5 visual result is the user's product direction; the user did not prescribe which class or constant should own it.
  - Codex technical choice: define one named cyan attack-telegraph color in the shared Blood Moon set-piece owner and use it for both warning and attack-VFX packets in Arena 3 and Arena 5, preserving the existing snow-aware mesh and approved fill, contour, sweep, arrows, impact core, and trail.
  - Follow-up inspection requested by the user: every Arena 3 and Arena 5 pattern is selected by the same set-piece loop, creates the same immutable `AttackGeometry` snapshot, sends warning through the same `EventNetwork.telegraph` call, sends impact through the same `EventNetwork.attackVfx` call, and is rendered by the same `EventAttackRenderer`; no Arena 3 particle-only or simplified bypass remains.

- Removed the client `/jem debug telegraph-render` command because the user explicitly required the telegraph toggle to be removed and telegraphs to remain always enabled. The user's technical direction was complete removal of that command and unconditional presentation; Codex independently removed its logging-only state, vertex-count bookkeeping, shader-pack-name reflection, and command documentation while preserving packet lifetime, dimension filtering, and shadow-pass exclusion as the only legitimate rendering conditions. The command did not actually own telegraph visibility, so no replacement switch or configuration was introduced.

- `Bug / Client / structure-boss boundaries produced a second distant cube that followed the camera / in testing`
  - Five whys: the extra cube moved with the view because the boundary renderer submitted world geometry while Oculus was rendering from its shadow camera; it submitted there because Forge emits the same world render stage during the shader shadow pass; the boundary callback accepted every `AFTER_TRANSLUCENT_BLOCKS` event without distinguishing the active framebuffer; the attack renderer already rejected this pass but its Oculus state detector was private to that renderer; the concrete root cause was that the shared event-boundary material had no shadow-pass lifecycle guard and was therefore transformed once for the player camera and again for the shader's shadow camera.
  - User request and technical direction: the user reported that structure bosses had the ordinary boundary plus a second cube in the distance attached to their view and required the incorrect cube to be removed. The user supplied the exact visual symptom but did not prescribe a rendering API or implementation.
  - Codex technical choice: reuse the existing optional Oculus shadow-pass detector already proven by attack telegraphs, expose only its shadow query within the client render package, and skip boundary submission before creating a buffer in that pass so the real world-space structure boundary remains unchanged in the normal color pass.

- `Bug / Client / Blood Moon boundary had no visible configured color with the shader-compatible material / in testing`
  - Five whys: the red tint disappeared because every boundary vertex declared an upward-facing floor normal; it declared that normal because the boundary renderer copied the vertex contract from horizontal attack tiles; Blood Moon geometry is instead a set of vertical terrain-following walls and rails; shader packs use the supplied normal when mapping entity-translucent-emissive vertices into their world buffers even when vanilla full-bright data is present; the concrete root cause was a geometry/material contract mismatch in which vertical boundary quads were submitted with `(0, 1, 0)` normals rather than normals perpendicular to each wall.
  - User request and technical direction: the user reported that the Blood Moon border had no color at all and required its event color to be visible. The user did not prescribe shader calculations, normal generation, brightness, or a replacement color.
  - Codex technical choice: preserve the configured `bloodMoonColor`, existing opacity pulse, full perimeter, terrain projection and vanilla-compatible material; derive one normalized horizontal normal from every wall segment and submit it with all four vertices, while also excluding the Oculus shadow pass shared with the structure-boundary defect.

- `Bug / Client / attack telegraphs were hidden whenever a snow layer covered the solid arena floor / in testing`
  - Five whys: warning and impact tiles disappeared on snow because their cached Y coordinate remained inside the snow volume; it remained inside the snow because the renderer added only a fixed `0.025` offset to `MOTION_BLOCKING_NO_LEAVES`; that heightmap ignored the non-motion-blocking snow layer and returned the top of the solid block beneath it; the snow then wrote a nearer world-depth value than the embedded telegraph quad; the concrete root cause was that coarse integer terrain height was never refined with the visible overlay block's fractional support shape before the depth-tested surface mesh was cached.
  - User report and technical direction: the user reported that attacks were visible only when the solid block had nothing above it and specifically identified snow as the covering case. The user approved the attack visuals themselves as beautiful and requested no redesign or technical implementation method.
  - Codex technical choice: preserve the approved fill, outline, sweep, impact front and shader-safe render material; refine only each cached cell's Y coordinate by reading the block support shape at the motion-height position and adding its exact maximum Y, which places the same visual above one through eight snow layers without changing server-owned damage geometry.

- `Bug / Client / Blood Moon and Resource Rush rendered only a one-block boundary fragment instead of the complete four-chunk perimeter / in testing`
  - Five whys: only the nearest fragment appeared because the client rejected every perimeter segment farther than `boundaryViewDistance + 4`; most segments were farther because the event packet begins being sent when only the nearest point of the area enters the same configured distance; this mismatch was especially severe near a corner because server visibility used distance to the rectangle while segment visibility used a separate Euclidean circle around the camera; the renderer therefore discarded geometry belonging to a zone the server had already declared visible; the concrete root cause was duplicated, incompatible visibility ownership between server-side whole-zone selection and client-side per-block culling.
  - User request and technical direction: the user required the complete Blood Moon and Resource Rush boundary to be visible across all four chunks and reported the exact observed result of one red block-wide, roughly two-block-high fragment. The user deliberately left the rendering mechanism to Codex rather than prescribing a technical implementation.
  - Codex technical choice: retain the server's whole-zone distance decision as the single visibility owner; remove per-segment distance rejection; move event boundaries from the immediate position-color pass to the existing shader-safe color-only material; and render a continuous terrain-following curtain with connected top/bottom rails and vertical scale markers every four blocks.

- `Bug / Client / telegraph ground fill disappeared with Photon or removed terrain pixels with Complementary, while tall cyan columns obscured the arena / in testing`
  - Five whys: the danger floor was missing or destructive because the telegraph used an immediate `Tesselator` `POSITION_COLOR` pass; that pass manually mutated global blend, depth, cull, and shader state; those states were outside a vanilla material contract that Oculus can consistently remap into shader-pack gbuffers; shader packs therefore either rejected the raw fill or interpreted it as a depth-affecting surface; the root cause was the rendering backend, not the selected particle color or a specific shader-pack defect.
  - User request and technical direction: use ground-projected fill and outline rather than particle columns, render after terrain depth exists, read depth without writing it, use normal alpha blending, keep a 0.01–0.03 block offset, avoid custom GLSL and direct framebuffer/OpenGL control, provide valid UV/light/normal data, skip shadow passes, and expose `/jem debug telegraph-render`.
  - Codex technical choice: add a dedicated `TelegraphRenderType` built from the vanilla entity-translucent-emissive shader contract and Minecraft's white texture; submit cached surface quads through `MultiBufferSource`; enforce `LEQUAL` depth test plus color-only writes; replace all vertical walls with bright flat edge strips and sparse flat direction arrows; use the inspected Oculus 1.8.0 API reflectively so the optional client mod never becomes a server classloading dependency.

- `Bug / Both Sided / custom attacks had a warning snapshot but no separate geometry-matched attack animation after warning expiry / in testing`
  - Five whys: players saw damage without a readable attacking force because impact VFX was an instantaneous server particle scatter; it was instantaneous because the network carried only the warning snapshot; without an attack-phase packet the client had no authoritative geometry to animate after warning removal; reusing warning lifetime left both stages visually merged; the missing contract was a distinct attack VFX snapshot using the same immutable server-owned damage geometry.
  - User request and technical direction: enforce wind-up → ground telegraph → final warning → telegraph removal → attack VFX → damage/impact → recovery for Arena 3 and Arena 5 custom patterns, with moving wave fronts and no return to particle forests.
  - Codex technical choice: add a protocol-versioned `AttackVfx` packet; remove the matching telegraph as soon as it arrives; cache the same surface mesh; derive a bounded per-cell activation phase from `AttackGeometry`; and animate a high-contrast travelling ground front for 12–18 ticks while retaining the existing short server impact particles as accents.

- `Bug / Server / Arena 5 boss-owned attacks retained their previous damage because the shared damage owner had no Arena 5 post-scaling multiplier / in testing`
  - Five whys: damage did not become 100% higher because Wave 5 still used only its existing `2.10` boss coefficient; changing individual attacks would miss native melee and projectiles; those paths differ before damage but converge in `LivingDamageEvent` through their canonical boss owner; signature attacks deliberately skip the ordinary coefficient to prevent duplicate scaling; therefore the only single-application point shared by melee, projectiles, explosions, AOE, waves, and signature damage is the set-piece boss branch in `EventMobModifiers`.
  - User request and technical direction: all Arena 5 boss damage must be exactly doubled from its current value through one centralized `2.0` modifier, excluding unrelated environmental damage and avoiding double application.
  - Codex technical choice: apply `2.0` once after ordinary/signature differentiation in the canonical damage event and scale the previous 35% safety cap to 70%, preserving the cap-to-damage ratio so strong hits are not silently clipped back to their old effective damage.

### Fixed

- `Bug / Client / attack telegraphs remained visually noisy and did not communicate danger shape, countdown, and impact as separate readable states / resolved`
  - Five whys: the result was hard to play from because every occupied block used nearly the same solid quad; those quads communicated occupancy but not timing because countdown was represented mainly by global alpha pulsing; impact still resembled the warning because both phases reused the same color hierarchy and cell treatment; those roles were never visually separated because the previous change concentrated on shader compatibility and authoritative geometry transport; the concrete root cause was the absence of a visual grammar assigning distinct marks to danger extent, activation timing, direction, and the damaging front.
  - User request and technical direction: the user required telegraphs to become beautiful, playable, and immediately understandable, without prescribing colors, layer counts, animation math, or a rendering API. The product direction is the user's; the concrete visual system is Codex's independent technical choice.
  - Codex technical choice: keep the full danger shape as a low-opacity inset tile field; add a dark support stroke plus a narrow light edge for safe/danger separation; animate a light activation sweep through the immutable server geometry; preserve sparse directional chevrons; and replace the impact fill with a white-hot moving core and short fading trail so warning and damage cannot be confused.
  - Resolution: the user explicitly approved the resulting attacks as beautiful; the issue is resolved through the separate low-opacity danger fill, double contour, activation sweep, directional markers, and white-hot moving impact front with a fading trail.

- Opening a player profile no longer mutates the live player entity for its 3D preview, preventing camera and cursor-like snapping while retaining vanilla skin rendering.
- Administrators can now end every active SMP event through one cleanup path, and Blood Moon rewards no longer contain experience bottles.
- Boss structures now publish persistent client-localized server landmarks after a player approaches within ten blocks, active events publish shared server markers for their lifetime, and the combat HUD wraps complete localized boss objectives instead of truncating them.
- Shop, Party, profile and territory map previews now create map-session-only local markers removed on Back or Escape, while shop actions use equal-width controls and centered button content.
- Territory permissions now use one flat toggle screen with an all-on/all-off control and only containers, fire, PvP, explosions, outside penetration, block placement, block breaking and beds. Anvils, enchanting tables, doors, gates, trapdoors, buttons, levers and note blocks are always usable, while vanilla and modded storage share the single container permission.
- Blood Moon enemies now belong to a specific combat attempt and are removed after defeat, including entities reloaded later from chunks.
- Blood Moon resources from a failed attempt are invalidated and removed from inventories, Ender Chests, dropped items, loaded containers and containers loaded later.
- Blue ice and compatible modded ice blocks placed inside event arenas evaporate after one to two seconds with sound and particles.
- Boss Raid and Boss Fight defeats now tell participants that the encounter failed and explain how to retry it.
- Boss-structure boundaries no longer support snow or player-placed blocks.
- Boss-structure roofs reject incoming water and lava, and pistons can no longer move blocks across the boundary.
